=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer array nums and a positive integer k. Return the sum of the maximum and minimum elements of all subarrays with at most k elements.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [1,2,3], k = 2
Output: 20
Explanation:
The subarrays of nums with at most 2 elements are:



Subarray
Minimum
Maximum
Sum


[1]
1
1
2


[2]
2
2
4


[3]
3
3
6


[1, 2]
1
2
3


[2, 3]
2
3
5


Final Total
 
 
20



The output would be 20.

Example 2:

Input: nums = [1,-3,1], k = 2
Output: -6
Explanation:
The subarrays of nums with at most 2 elements are:



Subarray
Minimum
Maximum
Sum


[1]
1
1
2


[-3]
-3
-3
-6


[1]
1
1
2


[1, -3]
-3
1
-2


[-3, 1]
-3
1
-2


Final Total
 
 
-6



The output would be -6.

 
Constraints:

1 <= nums.length <= 80000
1 <= k <= nums.length
-10^6 <= nums[i] <= 10^6

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Let's think about code style:
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minMaxSubarraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        def tri(s: int) -> int:
            if s >= 2:
                return s * (s - 1) // 2
            return 0

        def count_pairs(a: int, b: int, k: int) -> int:
            t = k + 1
            return tri(t) - tri(t - a) - tri(t - b) + tri(t - a - b)

        def min_sum(arr: List[int]) -> int:
            n = len(arr)
            prev = [-1] * n
            st = []
            for i, x in enumerate(arr):
                while st and arr[st[-1]] >= x:
                    st.pop()
                prev[i] = st[-1] if st else -1
                st.append(i)

            nxt = [n] * n
            st = []
            for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
                x = arr[i]
                while st and arr[st[-1]] > x:
                    st.pop()
                nxt[i] = st[-1] if st else n
                st.append(i)

            total = 0
            for i, x in enumerate(arr):
                a = i - prev[i]
                b = nxt[i] - i
                total += x * count_pairs(a, b, k)
            return total

        return min_sum(nums) - min_sum([-x for x in nums])
```

Let's reason with standard monotonic stack property: When scanning right to left and popping while value > x, the stack after popping contains indices with values <= x? Actually if top > x, pop; continue. The first index with value <= x is the nearest to the right with value <= x because any index between current and that index either was popped (and thus has a later index with <= value, which would be even closer? Wait "later" means to the right, not closer. Need be careful). Let's try to find a counterexample where nearest <= current is popped before current by a farther element? Is that possible? Popping happens when processing an index m to the left of j? Actually j can only be popped when processing some index p < j (to the left of j). At that time, p is closer to future current i < p than j. If p pops j, then nums[p] <= nums[j] (because pop while stack top > current? Wait in right-to-left scan, while stack top value > x, pop. So j is popped if nums[j] > nums[p]. Thus nums[p] < nums[j]. If nums[j] <= current, then nums[p] < nums[j] <= current, so p is also <= current and closer to current than j. Thus j is not needed. If p is to the right of current? p is between current and j. So nearest <= current is at least as close as p, not j. Thus safe. Good.

Now, let's think about if `prev_less` stack pop `>=` similarly safe. Good.

Since min_sum on negated uses same comparators, it counts rightmost minimum of negated. For original max, this corresponds to rightmost maximum? Let's see original [1,2,2], max of subarray [0,2] is 2 at indices1,2. Rightmost max index2. Negated [-1,-2,-2], min of subarray is -2 at indices1,2. Rightmost min of negated index2. Good. Sum unaffected.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with `count_pairs` if `k` is large and `t-a-b` is positive but `tri` returns a value that makes formula count > a*b? We tested. Let's prove with inclusion-exclusion. The number of positive pairs with x+y<=T and x<=A,y<=B. Let U = # positive pairs x+y<=T = tri(T). Let X = # with x>A = # u>=1,y>=1, u+y <= T-A = tri(T-A) (if T-A>=2). Let Y similarly. Intersection = # u,v>=1, u+v <= T-A-B = tri(T-A-B). Then count = U - X - Y + XY. This is exact. Good.

Before final, let's maybe think if there is a way to make code more self-contained:
```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minMaxSubarraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        def tri(s: int) -> int:
            return s * (s - 1) // 2 if s > 1 else 0

        def count(a: int, b: int) -> int:
            t = k + 1
            return tri(t) - tri(t - a) - tri(t - b) + tri(t - a - b)

        def min_sum(arr: List[int]) -> int:
            n = len(arr)
            prev = [-1] * n
            st = []
            for i, x in enumerate(arr):
                while st and arr[st[-1]] >= x:
                    st.pop()
                prev[i] = st[-1] if st else -1
                st.append(i)

            nxt = [n] * n
            st = []
            for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
                x = arr[i]
                while st and arr[st[-1]] > x:
                    st.pop()
                nxt[i] = st[-1] if st else n
                st.append(i)

            total = 0
            for i, x in enumerate(arr):
                total += x * count(i - prev[i], nxt[i] - i)
            return total

        return min_sum(nums) - min_sum([-x for x in nums])
```
This is concise. Note `count` closes over `k`. Good.

Potential issue: `count` function uses `k` from outer method; if we call min_sum twice, same k. Good.

Array [2,1,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top1>1 no, nxt=2? Wait top is index2 val1, value1 <=1, so next <=1 is i2 val1 (equal), but nearest <=1 to right of i1 is i2 val1, yes. push [3,2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,2,1]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val1 push
i2 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top2>0 pop, top1>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,0,2]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val2 push
i2 val0 top2>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [2]
i1 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=2 push [2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `prev_less` stack pop `>=` and previous strictly less if there is a smaller element that was popped by an even smaller later? We argued safe. Let's test [1,0,2] for i2 val2 prev_less should i1 val0. Stack after i1: i0 val1 push, i1 val0 pop i0, push i1. Stack [1]. i2 val2 top0>=2 no prev1. Good.
[1,2,0,3] for i3 val3 prev_less should i2 val0. Stack after i2: i0 val1 push, i1 val2 push, i2 val0 pop i1, pop i0, push i2. Stack [2]. i3 val3 prev2. Good.
[2,1,3,2] for i3 val2 prev_less should i1 val1. Stack after i2: i0 val2 push, i1 val1 pop i0 push1, i2 val3 push [1,2]. i3 val2 pop i2, top i1 val1>=2 no prev1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "at most k elements" and using `count_pairs` for each i as rightmost min. Suppose a subarray has rightmost min i, but there is an equal to left. Our `prev_less` ignores equal, so L can be after equal? Actually if equal to left within subarray, L <= equal < i. Condition L > prev_less. Since prev_less is nearest strictly smaller, not equal. If there is equal to left, prev_less could be before L or none. So L can be before equal. That's fine; i is rightmost min, equal to left allowed. The count includes all L in (prev_less, i]. It may include L positions that are before an equal to left? Yes. For subarray [0,1] in [1,1], i1 prev_less=-1, L can be0 or1; L=0 includes equal to left. Good.
Now, if there is an equal to left but also a smaller before L, prev_less < L. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "next_less_equal" if there is an equal to right outside subarray but before R? If equal to right within subarray, then i not rightmost min, so not counted. Condition R < next_less_equal ensures no equal to right in subarray. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "sum of maximum and minimum elements" if values are floats? No, ints.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with code if `nums` is a tuple and `[-x for x in nums]` returns list; okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "k" being a positive integer but maybe larger than n? Constraint says <=. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "nums.length <= 80000" and recursion not used. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "Return the sum" and no modulo; Python int. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] <= 10^6" and answer maybe > 2^63? Let's compute max number subarrays length<=n = n(n+1)/2 = 3.2e9. Each min+max can be up to 2e6 if values all positive? Actually if all positive 1e6, min+max=2e6 per subarray, total ~6.4e15. If values can be negative, min+max could be up to 2e6 (if min=-1e6,max=1e6) or down to -2e6. Absolute <=2e6. So <=6.4e15 <9.22e18. Fits 64-bit. Python fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "sum of the maximum and minimum elements" maybe they want sum of (max + min) for each subarray, not sum of max plus sum of min? Same.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with using `min_sum` on negated array for max if values are at bounds -10^6, 10^6; no overflow in Python. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "subarrays with at most k elements" and our count_pairs counts subarrays where i is min but length <=k. However, if there are multiple minima, the rightmost min is counted. Does the length constraint depend on chosen i? For a subarray, length is fixed. If rightmost min i, x+y-1 = length. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prev_less" and "next_less_equal" boundaries if there are values equal to current outside subarray but within boundaries. For rightmost min, next_less_equal > R, so no equal to right. Equal to left allowed. prev_less < L, so no smaller to left; equal to left not considered. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "previous strictly less" stack pop `>=` and "next less or equal" stack pop `>` if values are equal and we want rightmost min. We verified.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with `count_pairs` using `a = i - prev[i]` and `b = nxt[i] - i`. For i=0, prev=-1, a=1. For i=n-1, nxt=n, b=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `nxt` array from right-to-left: We initialize `nxt = [n] * n`, then for each i, if stack empty, nxt[i]=n. If stack top after popping has value <= x, nxt[i]=st[-1]. But what if there is a closer index with value <= x that is not in stack because it was popped by an even smaller value to its right? i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.

i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top1>1 no, nxt=2? Wait top is index2 val1, value1 <=1, so next <=1 is i2 val1 (equal), but nearest <=1 to right of i1 is i2 val1, yes. push [3,2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.

i0 val3 top1>3 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top1>0 pop, top0>0 no, nxt=3? Wait nearest <=0 to right of i1 is i3 val0, index3. But there is also i2 val1 not <=0. So nxt=3. push [3,1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top1>1 no, nxt=2? Wait top is index2 val1, value1 <=1, so next <=1 is i2 val1 (equal), but nearest <=1 to right of i1 is i2 val1, yes. push [3,2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,2,1]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val1 push
i2 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top2>0 pop, top1>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,0,2]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val2 push
i2 val0 top2>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [2]
i1 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=2 push [2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `prev_less` stack pop `>=` and previous strictly less if there is a smaller element that was popped by an even smaller later? We argued safe. Let's test [1,0,2] for i2 val2 prev_less should i1 val0. Stack after i1: i0 val1 push, i1 val0 pop i0, push i1. Stack [1]. i2 val2 top0>=2 no prev1. Good.
[1,2,0,3] for i3 val3 prev_less should i2 val0. Stack after i2: i0 val1 push, i1 val2 push, i2 val0 pop i1, pop i0, push i2. Stack [2]. i3 val3 prev2. Good.
[2,1,3,2] for i3 val2 prev_less should i1 val1. Stack after i2: i0 val2 push, i1 val1 pop i0 push1, i2 val3 push [1,2]. i3 val2 pop i2, top i1 val1>=2 no prev1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "at most k elements" and using `count_pairs` for each i as rightmost min. Suppose a subarray has rightmost min i, but there is an equal to left. Our `prev_less` ignores equal, so L can be after equal? Actually if equal to left within subarray, L <= equal < i. Condition L > prev_less. Since prev_less is nearest strictly smaller, not equal. If there is equal to left, prev_less could be before L or none. So L can be before equal. That's fine; i is rightmost min, equal to left allowed. The count includes all L in (prev_less, i]. It may include L positions that are before an equal to left? Yes. For subarray [0,1] in [1,1], i1 prev_less=-1, L can be0 or1; L=0 includes equal to left. Good.
Now, if there is an equal to left but also a smaller before L, prev_less < L. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "next_less_equal" if there is an equal to right outside subarray but before R? If equal to right within subarray, then i not rightmost min, so not counted. Condition R < next_less_equal ensures no equal to right in subarray. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "sum of maximum and minimum elements" if values are floats? No, ints.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with code if `nums` is a tuple and `[-x for x in nums]` returns list; okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "k" being a positive integer but maybe larger than n? Constraint says <=. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "nums.length <= 80000" and recursion not used. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "Return the sum" and no modulo; Python int. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] <= 10^6" and answer maybe > 2^63? Let's compute max number subarrays length<=n = n(n+1)/2 = 3.2e9. Each min+max can be up to 2e6 if values all positive? Actually if all positive 1e6, min+max=2e6 per subarray, total ~6.4e15. If values can be negative, min+max could be up to 2e6 (if min=-1e6,max=1e6) or down to -2e6. Absolute <=2e6. So <=6.4e15 <9.22e18. Fits 64-bit. Python fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "sum of the maximum and minimum elements" maybe they want sum of (max + min) for each subarray, not sum of max plus sum of min? Same.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with using `min_sum` on negated array for max if values are at bounds -10^6, 10^6; no overflow in Python. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "subarrays with at most k elements" and our count_pairs counts subarrays where i is min but length <=k. However, if there are multiple minima, the rightmost min is counted. Does the length constraint depend on chosen i? For a subarray, length is fixed. If rightmost min i, x+y-1 = length. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prev_less" and "next_less_equal" boundaries if there are values equal to current outside subarray but within boundaries. For rightmost min, next_less_equal > R, so no equal to right. Equal to left allowed. prev_less < L, so no smaller to left; equal to left not considered. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "previous strictly less" stack pop `>=` and "next less or equal" stack pop `>` if values are equal and we want rightmost min. We verified.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with `count_pairs` using `a = i - prev[i]` and `b = nxt[i] - i`. For i=0, prev=-1, a=1. For i=n-1, nxt=n, b=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `nxt` array from right-to-left: We initialize `nxt = [n] * n`, then for each i, if stack empty, nxt[i]=n. If stack top after popping has value <= x, nxt[i]=st[-1]. But what if there is a closer index with value <= x that is not in stack because it was popped by an even smaller value to its right? i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.

If stack top after popping has value <= x, nxt[i]=st[-1]. But what if there is a closer index with value <= x that is not in stack because it was popped by an even smaller value to its right? We argued safe. Let's try to find a concrete counterexample with brute mentally:
Array [3,1,2,0]. For i0 val3, next <=3 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push [3]
i2 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top2>1 pop, top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,1]
i0 val3 top1>3 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top1>0 pop, top0>0 no, nxt=3? Wait nearest <=0 to right of i1 is i3 val0, index3. But there is also i2 val1 not <=0. So nxt=3. push [3,1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top1>1 no, nxt=2? Wait top is index2 val1, value1 <=1, so next <=1 is i2 val1 (equal), but nearest <=1 to right of i1 is i2 val1, yes. push [3,2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,2,1]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val1 push
i2 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top2>0 pop, top1>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,0,2]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val2 push
i2 val0 top2>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [2]
i1 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=2 push [2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `prev_less` stack pop `>=` and previous strictly less if there is a smaller element that was popped by an even smaller later? We argued safe. Let's test [1,0,2] for i2 val2 prev_less should i1 val0. Stack after i1: i0 val1 push, i1 val0 pop i0, push i1. Stack [1]. i2 val2 top0>=2 no prev1. Good.
[1,2,0,3] for i3 val3 prev_less should i2 val0. Stack after i2: i0 val1 push, i1 val2 push, i2 val0 pop i1, pop i0, push i2. Stack [2]. i3 val3 prev2. Good.
[2,1,3,2] for i3 val2 prev_less should i1 val1. Stack after i2: i0 val2 push, i1 val1 pop i0 push1, i2 val3 push [1,2]. i3 val2 pop i2, top i1 val1>=2 no prev1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "at most k elements" and using `count_pairs` for each i as rightmost min. Suppose a subarray has rightmost min i, but there is an equal to left. Our `prev_less` ignores equal, so L can be after equal? Actually if equal to left within subarray, L <= equal < i. Condition L > prev_less. Since prev_less is nearest strictly smaller, not equal. If there is equal to left, prev_less could be before L or none. So L can be before equal. That's fine; i is rightmost min, equal to left allowed. The count includes all L in (prev_less, i]. It may include L positions that are before an equal to left? Yes. For subarray [0,1] in [1,1], i1 prev_less=-1, L can be0 or1; L=0 includes equal to left. Good.
Now, if there is an equal to left but also a smaller before L, prev_less < L. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "next_less_equal" if there is an equal to right outside subarray but before R? If equal to right within subarray, then i not rightmost min, so not counted. Condition R < next_less_equal ensures no equal to right in subarray. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "sum of maximum and minimum elements" if values are floats? No, ints.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with code if `nums` is a tuple and `[-x for x in nums]` returns list; okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "k" being a positive integer but maybe larger than n? Constraint says <=. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "nums.length <= 80000" and recursion not used. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "Return the sum" and no modulo; Python int. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] <= 10^6" and answer maybe > 2^63? Let's compute max number subarrays length<=n = n(n+1)/2 = 3.2e9. Each min+max can be up to 2e6 if values all positive? Actually if all positive 1e6, min+max=2e6 per subarray, total ~6.4e15. If values can be negative, min+max could be up to 2e6 (if min=-1e6,max=1e6) or down to -2e6. Absolute <=2e6. So <=6.4e15 <9.22e18. Fits 64-bit. Python fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "sum of the maximum and minimum elements" maybe they want sum of (max + min) for each subarray, not sum of max plus sum of min? Same.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with using `min_sum` on negated array for max if values are at bounds -10^6, 10^6; no overflow in Python. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "subarrays with at most k elements" and our count_pairs counts subarrays where i is min but length <=k. However, if there are multiple minima, the rightmost min is counted. Does the length constraint depend on chosen i? For a subarray, length is fixed. If rightmost min i, x+y-1 = length. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prev_less" and "next_less_equal" boundaries if there are values equal to current outside subarray but within boundaries. For rightmost min, next_less_equal > R, so no equal to right. Equal to left allowed. prev_less < L, so no smaller to left; equal to left not considered. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "previous strictly less" stack pop `>=` and "next less or equal" stack pop `>` if values are equal and we want rightmost min. We verified.

For rightmost min, next_less_equal > R, so no equal to right. Equal to left allowed. prev_less < L, so no smaller to left; equal to left not considered. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "previous strictly less" stack pop `>=` and "next less or equal" stack pop `>` if values are equal and we want rightmost min. We verified.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with `count_pairs` using `a = i - prev[i]` and `b = nxt[i] - i`. For i=0, prev=-1, a=1. For i=n-1, nxt=n, b=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `nxt` array from right-to-left: We initialize `nxt = [n] * n`, then for each i, if stack empty, nxt[i]=n. If stack top after popping has value <= x, nxt[i]=st[-1]. But what if there is a closer index with value <= x that is not in stack because it was popped by an even smaller value to its right? We argued safe. Let's try to find a concrete counterexample with brute mentally:
Array [3,1,2,0]. For i0 val3, next <=3 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push [3]
i2 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top2>1 pop, top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,1]
i0 val3 top1>3 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top1>0 pop, top0>0 no, nxt=3? Wait nearest <=0 to right of i1 is i3 val0, index3. But there is also i2 val1 not <=0. So nxt=3. push [3,1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top1>1 no, nxt=2? Wait top is index2 val1, value1 <=1, so next <=1 is i2 val1 (equal), but nearest <=1 to right of i1 is i2 val1, yes. push [3,2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,2,1]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val1 push
i2 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top2>0 pop, top1>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,0,2]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val2 push
i2 val0 top2>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [2]
i1 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=2 push [2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `prev_less` stack pop `>=` and previous strictly less if there is a smaller element that was popped by an even smaller later? We argued safe. Let's test [1,0,2] for i2 val2 prev_less should i1 val0. Stack after i1: i0 val1 push, i1 val0 pop i0, push i1. Stack [1]. i2 val2 top0>=2 no prev1. Good.
[1,2,0,3] for i3 val3 prev_less should i2 val0. Stack after i2: i0 val1 push, i1 val2 push, i2 val0 pop i1, pop i0, push i2. Stack [2]. i3 val3 prev2. Good.
[2,1,3,2] for i3 val2 prev_less should i1 val1. Stack after i2: i0 val2 push, i1 val1 pop i0 push1, i2 val3 push [1,2]. i3 val2 pop i2, top i1 val1>=2 no prev1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "at most k elements" and using `count_pairs` for each i as rightmost min. Suppose a subarray has rightmost min i, but there is an equal to left. Our `prev_less` ignores equal, so L can be after equal? Actually if equal to left within subarray, L <= equal < i. Condition L > prev_less. Since prev_less is nearest strictly smaller, not equal. If there is equal to left, prev_less could be before L or none. So L can be before equal. That's fine; i is rightmost min, equal to left allowed. The count includes all L in (prev_less, i]. It may include L positions that are before an equal to left? Yes. For subarray [0,1] in [1,1], i1 prev_less=-1, L can be0 or1; L=0 includes equal to left. Good.
Now, if there is an equal to left but also a smaller before L, prev_less < L. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "next_less_equal" if there is an equal to right outside subarray but before R? If equal to right within subarray, then i not rightmost min, so not counted. Condition R < next_less_equal ensures no equal to right in subarray. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "sum of maximum and minimum elements" if values are floats? No, ints.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with code if `nums` is a tuple and `[-x for x in nums]` returns list; okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "k" being a positive integer but maybe larger than n? Constraint says <=. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "nums.length <= 80000" and recursion not used. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "Return the sum" and no modulo; Python int. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] <= 10^6" and answer maybe > 2^63? Let's compute max number subarrays length<=n = n(n+1)/2 = 3.2e9. Each min+max can be up to 2e6 if values all positive? Actually if all positive 1e6, min+max=2e6 per subarray, total ~6.4e15. If values can be negative, min+max could be up to 2e6 (if min=-1e6,max=1e6) or down to -2e6. Absolute <=2e6. So <=6.4e15 <9.22e18. Fits 64-bit. Python fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "sum of the maximum and minimum elements" maybe they want sum of (max + min) for each subarray, not sum of max plus sum of min? Same.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with using `min_sum` on negated array for max if values are at bounds -10^6, 10^6; no overflow in Python. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with using `min_sum` on negated array for max if values are at bounds -10^6, 10^6; no overflow in Python. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "subarrays with at most k elements" and our count_pairs counts subarrays where i is min but length <=k. However, if there are multiple minima, the rightmost min is counted. Does the length constraint depend on chosen i? For a subarray, length is fixed. If rightmost min i, x+y-1 = length. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prev_less" and "next_less_equal" boundaries if there are values equal to current outside subarray but within boundaries. For rightmost min, next_less_equal > R, so no equal to right. Equal to left allowed. prev_less < L, so no smaller to left; equal to left not considered. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "previous strictly less" stack pop `>=` and "next less or equal" stack pop `>` if values are equal and we want rightmost min. We verified.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with `count_pairs` using `a = i - prev[i]` and `b = nxt[i] - i`. For i=0, prev=-1, a=1. For i=n-1, nxt=n, b=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `nxt` array from right-to-left: We initialize `nxt = [n] * n`, then for each i, if stack empty, nxt[i]=n. If stack top after popping has value <= x, nxt[i]=st[-1]. But what if there is a closer index with value <= x that is not in stack because it was popped by an even smaller value to its right? We argued safe. Let's try to find a concrete counterexample with brute mentally:
Array [3,1,2,0]. For i0 val3, next <=3 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push [3]
i2 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top2>1 pop, top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,1]
i0 val3 top1>3 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top1>0 pop, top0>0 no, nxt=3? Wait nearest <=0 to right of i1 is i3 val0, index3. But there is also i2 val1 not <=0. So nxt=3. push [3,1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top1>1 no, nxt=2? Wait top is index2 val1, value1 <=1, so next <=1 is i2 val1 (equal), but nearest <=1 to right of i1 is i2 val1, yes. push [3,2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,2,1]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val1 push
i2 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top2>0 pop, top1>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,0,2]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val2 push
i2 val0 top2>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [2]
i1 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=2 push [2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `prev_less` stack pop `>=` and previous strictly less if there is a smaller element that was popped by an even smaller later? We argued safe. Let's test [1,0,2] for i2 val2 prev_less should i1 val0. Stack after i1: i0 val1 push, i1 val0 pop i0, push i1. Stack [1]. i2 val2 top0>=2 no prev1. Good.
[1,2,0,3] for i3 val3 prev_less should i2 val0. Stack after i2: i0 val1 push, i1 val2 push, i2 val0 pop i1, pop i0, push i2. Stack [2]. i3 val3 prev2. Good.
[2,1,3,2] for i3 val2 prev_less should i1 val1. Stack after i2: i0 val2 push, i1 val1 pop i0 push1, i2 val3 push [1,2]. i3 val2 pop i2, top i1 val1>=2 no prev1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "at most k elements" and using `count_pairs` for each i as rightmost min. Suppose a subarray has rightmost min i, but there is an equal to left. Our `prev_less` ignores equal, so L can be after equal? Actually if equal to left within subarray, L <= equal < i. Condition L > prev_less. Since prev_less is nearest strictly smaller, not equal. If there is equal to left, prev_less could be before L or none. So L can be before equal. That's fine; i is rightmost min, equal to left allowed. The count includes all L in (prev_less, i]. It may include L positions that are before an equal to left? Yes. For subarray [0,1] in [1,1], i1 prev_less=-1, L can be0 or1; L=0 includes equal to left. Good.
Now, if there is an equal to left but also a smaller before L, prev_less < L. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "next_less_equal" if there is an equal to right outside subarray but before R? If equal to right within subarray, then i not rightmost min, so not counted. Condition R < next_less_equal ensures no equal to right in subarray. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "sum of maximum and minimum elements" if values are floats? No, ints.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with code if `nums` is a tuple and `[-x for x in nums]` returns list; okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "k" being a positive integer but maybe larger than n? Constraint says <=. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "nums.length <= 80000" and recursion not used. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "Return the sum" and no modulo; Python int. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] <= 10^6" and answer maybe > 2^63? Let's compute max number subarrays length<=n = n(n+1)/2 = 3.2e9. Each min+max can be up to 2e6 if values all positive? Actually if all positive 1e6, min+max=2e6 per subarray, total ~6.4e15. If values can be negative, min+max could be up to 2e6 (if min=-1e6,max=1e6) or down to -2e6. Absolute <=2e6. So <=6.4e15 <9.22e18. Fits 64-bit. Python fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "sum of the maximum and minimum elements" maybe they want sum of (max + min) for each subarray, not sum of max plus sum of min? Same.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with using `min_sum` on negated array for max if values are at bounds -10^6, 10^6; no overflow in Python. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "subarrays with at most k elements" and our count_pairs counts subarrays where i is min but length <=k. However, if there are multiple minima, the rightmost min is counted. Does the length constraint depend on chosen i? For a subarray, length is fixed. If rightmost min i, x+y-1 = length. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prev_less" and "next_less_equal" boundaries if there are values equal to current outside subarray but within boundaries. For rightmost min, next_less_equal > R, so no equal to right. Equal to left allowed. prev_less < L, so no smaller to left; equal to left not considered. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "previous strictly less" stack pop `>=` and "next less or equal" stack pop `>` if values are equal and we want rightmost min. We verified.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with `count_pairs` using `a = i - prev[i]` and `b = nxt[i] - i`. For i=0, prev=-1, a=1. For i=n-1, nxt=n, b=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `nxt` array from right-to-left: We initialize `nxt = [n] * n`, then for each i, if stack empty, nxt[i]=n. If stack top after popping has value <= x, nxt[i]=st[-1]. But what if there is a closer index with value <= x that is not in stack because it was popped by an even smaller value to its right? We argued safe. Let's try to find a concrete counterexample with brute mentally:
Array [3,1,2,0]. For i0 val3, next <=3 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push [3]
i2 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top2>1 pop, top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,1]
i0 val3 top1>3 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top1>0 pop, top0>0 no, nxt=3? Wait nearest <=0 to right of i1 is i3 val0, index3. But there is also i2 val1 not <=0. So nxt=3. push [3,1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top1>1 no, nxt=2? Wait top is index2 val1, value1 <=1, so next <=1 is i2 val1 (equal), but nearest <=1 to right of i1 is i2 val1, yes. push [3,2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,2,1]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val1 push
i2 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top2>0 pop, top1>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,0,2]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val2 push
i2 val0 top2>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [2]
i1 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=2 push [2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `prev_less` stack pop `>=` and previous strictly less if there is a smaller element that was popped by an even smaller later? We argued safe. Let's test [1,0,2] for i2 val2 prev_less should i1 val0. Stack after i1: i0 val1 push, i1 val0 pop i0, push i1. Stack [1]. i2 val2 top0>=2 no prev1. Good.
[1,2,0,3] for i3 val3 prev_less should i2 val0. Stack after i2: i0 val1 push, i1 val2 push, i2 val0 pop i1, pop i0, push i2. Stack [2]. i3 val3 prev2. Good.
[2,1,3,2] for i3 val2 prev_less should i1 val1. Stack after i2: i0 val2 push, i1 val1 pop i0 push1, i2 val3 push [1,2]. i3 val2 pop i2, top i1 val1>=2 no prev1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "at most k elements" and using `count_pairs` for each i as rightmost min. Suppose a subarray has rightmost min i, but there is an equal to left. Our `prev_less` ignores equal, so L can be after equal? Actually if equal to left within subarray, L <= equal < i. Condition L > prev_less. Since prev_less is nearest strictly smaller, not equal. If there is equal to left, prev_less could be before L or none. So L can be before equal. That's fine; i is rightmost min, equal to left allowed. The count includes all L in (prev_less, i]. It may include L positions that are before an equal to left? Yes. For subarray [0,1] in [1,1], i1 prev_less=-1, L can be0 or1; L=0 includes equal to left. Good.
Now, if there is an equal to left but also a smaller before L, prev_less < L. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "next_less_equal" if there is an equal to right outside subarray but before R? If equal to right within subarray, then i not rightmost min, so not counted. Condition R < next_less_equal ensures no equal to right in subarray. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with code if `nums` is a tuple and `[-x for x in nums]` returns list; okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "k" being a positive integer but maybe larger than n? Constraint says <=. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "nums.length <= 80000" and recursion not used. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "Return the sum" and no modulo; Python int. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] <= 10^6" and answer maybe > 2^63? Let's compute max number subarrays length<=n = n(n+1)/2 = 3.2e9. Each min+max can be up to 2e6 if values all positive? Actually if all positive 1e6, min+max=2e6 per subarray, total ~6.4e15. If values can be negative, min+max could be up to 2e6 (if min=-1e6,max=1e6) or down to -2e6. Absolute <=2e6. So <=6.4e15 <9.22e18. Fits 64-bit. Python fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "sum of the maximum and minimum elements" maybe they want sum of (max + min) for each subarray, not sum of max plus sum of min? Same.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with using `min_sum` on negated array for max if values are at bounds -10^6, 10^6; no overflow in Python. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "subarrays with at most k elements" and our count_pairs counts subarrays where i is min but length <=k. However, if there are multiple minima, the rightmost min is counted. Does the length constraint depend on chosen i? For a subarray, length is fixed. If rightmost min i, x+y-1 = length. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prev_less" and "next_less_equal" boundaries if there are values equal to current outside subarray but within boundaries. For rightmost min, next_less_equal > R, so no equal to right. Equal to left allowed. prev_less < L, so no smaller to left; equal to left not considered. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "previous strictly less" stack pop `>=` and "next less or equal" stack pop `>` if values are equal and we want rightmost min. We verified.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with `count_pairs` using `a = i - prev[i]` and `b = nxt[i] - i`. For i=0, prev=-1, a=1. For i=n-1, nxt=n, b=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `nxt` array from right-to-left: We initialize `nxt = [n] * n`, then for each i, if stack empty, nxt[i]=n. If stack top after popping has value <= x, nxt[i]=st[-1]. But what if there is a closer index with value <= x that is not in stack because it was popped by an even smaller value to its right? We argued safe. Let's try to find a concrete counterexample with brute mentally:
Array [3,1,2,0]. For i0 val3, next <=3 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push [3]
i2 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top2>1 pop, top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,1]
i0 val3 top1>3 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top1>0 pop, top0>0 no, nxt=3? Wait nearest <=0 to right of i1 is i3 val0, index3. But there is also i2 val1 not <=0. So nxt=3. push [3,1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top1>1 no, nxt=2? Wait top is index2 val1, value1 <=1, so next <=1 is i2 val1 (equal), but nearest <=1 to right of i1 is i2 val1, yes. push [3,2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,2,1]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val1 push
i2 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top2>0 pop, top1>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,0,2]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val2 push
i2 val0 top2>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [2]
i1 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=2 push [2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `prev_less` stack pop `>=` and previous strictly less if there is a smaller element that was popped by an even smaller later? We argued safe. Let's test [1,0,2] for i2 val2 prev_less should i1 val0. Stack after i1: i0 val1 push, i1 val0 pop i0, push i1. Stack [1]. i2 val2 top0>=2 no prev1. Good.
[1,2,0,3] for i3 val3 prev_less should i2 val0. Stack after i2: i0 val1 push, i1 val2 push, i2 val0 pop i1, pop i0, push i2. Stack [2]. i3 val3 prev2. Good.
[2,1,3,2] for i3 val2 prev_less should i1 val1. Stack after i2: i0 val2 push, i1 val1 pop i0 push1, i2 val3 push [1,2]. i3 val2 pop i2, top i1 val1>=2 no prev1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "at most k elements" and using `count_pairs` for each i as rightmost min. Suppose a subarray has rightmost min i, but there is an equal to left. Our `prev_less` ignores equal, so L can be after equal? Actually if equal to left within subarray, L <= equal < i. Condition L > prev_less. Since prev_less is nearest strictly smaller, not equal. If there is equal to left, prev_less could be before L or none. So L can be before equal. That's fine; i is rightmost min, equal to left allowed. The count includes all L in (prev_less, i]. It may include L positions that are before an equal to left? Yes. For subarray [0,1] in [1,1], i1 prev_less=-1, L can be0 or1; L=0 includes equal to left. Good.
Now, if there is an equal to left but also a smaller before L, prev_less < L. Good.

For subarray [0,1] in [1,1], i1 prev_less=-1, L can be0 or1; L=0 includes equal to left. Good.
Now, if there is an equal to left but also a smaller before L, prev_less < L. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "next_less_equal" if there is an equal to right outside subarray but before R? If equal to right within subarray, then i not rightmost min, so not counted. Condition R < next_less_equal ensures no equal to right in subarray. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "sum of maximum and minimum elements" if values are floats? No, ints.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with code if `nums` is a tuple and `[-x for x in nums]` returns list; okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "k" being a positive integer but maybe larger than n? Constraint says <=. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "nums.length <= 80000" and recursion not used. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "Return the sum" and no modulo; Python int. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] <= 10^6" and answer maybe > 2^63? Let's compute max number subarrays length<=n = n(n+1)/2 = 3.2e9. Each min+max can be up to 2e6 if values all positive? Actually if all positive 1e6, min+max=2e6 per subarray, total ~6.4e15. If values can be negative, min+max could be up to 2e6 (if min=-1e6,max=1e6) or down to -2e6. Absolute <=2e6. So <=6.4e15 <9.22e18. Fits 64-bit. Python fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "sum of the maximum and minimum elements" maybe they want sum of (max + min) for each subarray, not sum of max plus sum of min? Same.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with using `min_sum` on negated array for max if values are at bounds -10^6, 10^6; no overflow in Python. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "subarrays with at most k elements" and our count_pairs counts subarrays where i is min but length <=k. However, if there are multiple minima, the rightmost min is counted. Does the length constraint depend on chosen i? For a subarray, length is fixed. If rightmost min i, x+y-1 = length. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prev_less" and "next_less_equal" boundaries if there are values equal to current outside subarray but within boundaries. For rightmost min, next_less_equal > R, so no equal to right. Equal to left allowed. prev_less < L, so no smaller to left; equal to left not considered. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "previous strictly less" stack pop `>=` and "next less or equal" stack pop `>` if values are equal and we want rightmost min. We verified.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with `count_pairs` using `a = i - prev[i]` and `b = nxt[i] - i`. For i=0, prev=-1, a=1. For i=n-1, nxt=n, b=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `nxt` array from right-to-left: We initialize `nxt = [n] * n`, then for each i, if stack empty, nxt[i]=n. If stack top after popping has value <= x, nxt[i]=st[-1]. But what if there is a closer index with value <= x that is not in stack because it was popped by an even smaller value to its right? We argued safe. Let's try to find a concrete counterexample with brute mentally:
Array [3,1,2,0]. For i0 val3, next <=3 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push [3]
i2 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top2>1 pop, top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,1]
i0 val3 top1>3 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top1>0 pop, top0>0 no, nxt=3? Wait nearest <=0 to right of i1 is i3 val0, index3. But there is also i2 val1 not <=0. So nxt=3. push [3,1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top1>1 no, nxt=2? Wait top is index2 val1, value1 <=1, so next <=1 is i2 val1 (equal), but nearest <=1 to right of i1 is i2 val1, yes. push [3,2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,2,1]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val1 push
i2 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top2>0 pop, top1>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,0,2]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val2 push
i2 val0 top2>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [2]
i1 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=2 push [2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `prev_less` stack pop `>=` and previous strictly less if there is a smaller element that was popped by an even smaller later? We argued safe. Let's test [1,0,2] for i2 val2 prev_less should i1 val0. Stack after i1: i0 val1 push, i1 val0 pop i0, push i1. Stack [1]. i2 val2 top0>=2 no prev1. Good.
[1,2,0,3] for i3 val3 prev_less should i2 val0. Stack after i2: i0 val1 push, i1 val2 push, i2 val0 pop i1, pop i0, push i2. Stack [2]. i3 val3 prev2. Good.
[2,1,3,2] for i3 val2 prev_less should i1 val1. Stack after i2: i0 val2 push, i1 val1 pop i0 push1, i2 val3 push [1,2]. i3 val2 pop i2, top i1 val1>=2 no prev1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "at most k elements" and using `count_pairs` for each i as rightmost min. Suppose a subarray has rightmost min i, but there is an equal to left. Our `prev_less` ignores equal, so L can be after equal? Actually if equal to left within subarray, L <= equal < i. Condition L > prev_less. Since prev_less is nearest strictly smaller, not equal. If there is equal to left, prev_less could be before L or none. So L can be before equal. That's fine; i is rightmost min, equal to left allowed. The count includes all L in (prev_less, i]. It may include L positions that are before an equal to left? Yes. For subarray [0,1] in [1,1], i1 prev_less=-1, L can be0 or1; L=0 includes equal to left. Good.
Now, if there is an equal to left but also a smaller before L, prev_less < L. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "next_less_equal" if there is an equal to right outside subarray but before R? If equal to right within subarray, then i not rightmost min, so not counted. Condition R < next_less_equal ensures no equal to right in subarray. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "sum of maximum and minimum elements" if values are floats? No, ints.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with code if `nums` is a tuple and `[-x for x in nums]` returns list; okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "k" being a positive integer but maybe larger than n? Constraint says <=. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "nums.length <= 80000" and recursion not used. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "Return the sum" and no modulo; Python int. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] <= 10^6" and answer maybe > 2^63? Let's compute max number subarrays length<=n = n(n+1)/2 = 3.2e9. Each min+max can be up to 2e6 if values all positive? Actually if all positive 1e6, min+max=2e6 per subarray, total ~6.4e15. If values can be negative, min+max could be up to 2e6 (if min=-1e6,max=1e6) or down to -2e6. Absolute <=2e6. So <=6.4e15 <9.22e18. Fits 64-bit. Python fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "sum of the maximum and minimum elements" maybe they want sum of (max + min) for each subarray, not sum of max plus sum of min? Same.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with using `min_sum` on negated array for max if values are at bounds -10^6, 10^6; no overflow in Python. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "subarrays with at most k elements" and our count_pairs counts subarrays where i is min but length <=k. However, if there are multiple minima, the rightmost min is counted. Does the length constraint depend on chosen i? For a subarray, length is fixed. If rightmost min i, x+y-1 = length. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prev_less" and "next_less_equal" boundaries if there are values equal to current outside subarray but within boundaries. For rightmost min, next_less_equal > R, so no equal to right. Equal to left allowed. prev_less < L, so no smaller to left; equal to left not considered. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "previous strictly less" stack pop `>=` and "next less or equal" stack pop `>` if values are equal and we want rightmost min. We verified.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with `count_pairs` using `a = i - prev[i]` and `b = nxt[i] - i`. For i=0, prev=-1, a=1. For i=n-1, nxt=n, b=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `nxt` array from right-to-left: We initialize `nxt = [n] * n`, then for each i, if stack empty, nxt[i]=n. If stack top after popping has value <= x, nxt[i]=st[-1]. But what if there is a closer index with value <= x that is not in stack because it was popped by an even smaller value to its right? We argued safe. Let's try to find a concrete counterexample with brute mentally:
Array [3,1,2,0]. For i0 val3, next <=3 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push [3]
i2 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top2>1 pop, top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,1]
i0 val3 top1>3 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top1>0 pop, top0>0 no, nxt=3? Wait nearest <=0 to right of i1 is i3 val0, index3. But there is also i2 val1 not <=0. So nxt=3. push [3,1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top1>1 no, nxt=2? Wait top is index2 val1, value1 <=1, so next <=1 is i2 val1 (equal), but nearest <=1 to right of i1 is i2 val1, yes. push [3,2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,2,1]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val1 push
i2 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top2>0 pop, top1>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,0,2]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val2 push
i2 val0 top2>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [2]
i1 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=2 push [2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `prev_less` stack pop `>=` and previous strictly less if there is a smaller element that was popped by an even smaller later? We argued safe. Let's test [1,0,2] for i2 val2 prev_less should i1 val0. Stack after i1: i0 val1 push, i1 val0 pop i0, push i1. Stack [1]. i2 val2 top0>=2 no prev1. Good.
[1,2,0,3] for i3 val3 prev_less should i2 val0. Stack after i2: i0 val1 push, i1 val2 push, i2 val0 pop i1, pop i0, push i2. Stack [2]. i3 val3 prev2. Good.
[2,1,3,2] for i3 val2 prev_less should i1 val1. Stack after i2: i0 val2 push, i1 val1 pop i0 push1, i2 val3 push [1,2]. i3 val2 pop i2, top i1 val1>=2 no prev1. Good.

[1,2,0,3] for i3 val3 prev_less should i2 val0. Stack after i2: i0 val1 push, i1 val2 push, i2 val0 pop i1, pop i0, push i2. Stack [2]. i3 val3 prev2. Good.
[2,1,3,2] for i3 val2 prev_less should i1 val1. Stack after i2: i0 val2 push, i1 val1 pop i0 push1, i2 val3 push [1,2]. i3 val2 pop i2, top i1 val1>=2 no prev1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "at most k elements" and using `count_pairs` for each i as rightmost min. Suppose a subarray has rightmost min i, but there is an equal to left. Our `prev_less` ignores equal, so L can be after equal? Actually if equal to left within subarray, L <= equal < i. Condition L > prev_less. Since prev_less is nearest strictly smaller, not equal. If there is equal to left, prev_less could be before L or none. So L can be before equal. That's fine; i is rightmost min, equal to left allowed. The count includes all L in (prev_less, i]. It may include L positions that are before an equal to left? Yes. For subarray [0,1] in [1,1], i1 prev_less=-1, L can be0 or1; L=0 includes equal to left. Good.
Now, if there is an equal to left but also a smaller before L, prev_less < L. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "next_less_equal" if there is an equal to right outside subarray but before R? If equal to right within subarray, then i not rightmost min, so not counted. Condition R < next_less_equal ensures no equal to right in subarray. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "sum of maximum and minimum elements" if values are floats? No, ints.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with code if `nums` is a tuple and `[-x for x in nums]` returns list; okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "k" being a positive integer but maybe larger than n? Constraint says <=. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "nums.length <= 80000" and recursion not used. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "Return the sum" and no modulo; Python int. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] <= 10^6" and answer maybe > 2^63? Let's compute max number subarrays length<=n = n(n+1)/2 = 3.2e9. Each min+max can be up to 2e6 if values all positive? Actually if all positive 1e6, min+max=2e6 per subarray, total ~6.4e15. If values can be negative, min+max could be up to 2e6 (if min=-1e6,max=1e6) or down to -2e6. Absolute <=2e6. So <=6.4e15 <9.22e18. Fits 64-bit. Python fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "sum of the maximum and minimum elements" maybe they want sum of (max + min) for each subarray, not sum of max plus sum of min? Same.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with using `min_sum` on negated array for max if values are at bounds -10^6, 10^6; no overflow in Python. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "subarrays with at most k elements" and our count_pairs counts subarrays where i is min but length <=k. However, if there are multiple minima, the rightmost min is counted. Does the length constraint depend on chosen i? For a subarray, length is fixed. If rightmost min i, x+y-1 = length. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prev_less" and "next_less_equal" boundaries if there are values equal to current outside subarray but within boundaries. For rightmost min, next_less_equal > R, so no equal to right. Equal to left allowed. prev_less < L, so no smaller to left; equal to left not considered. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "previous strictly less" stack pop `>=` and "next less or equal" stack pop `>` if values are equal and we want rightmost min. We verified.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with `count_pairs` using `a = i - prev[i]` and `b = nxt[i] - i`. For i=0, prev=-1, a=1. For i=n-1, nxt=n, b=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `nxt` array from right-to-left: We initialize `nxt = [n] * n`, then for each i, if stack empty, nxt[i]=n. If stack top after popping has value <= x, nxt[i]=st[-1]. But what if there is a closer index with value <= x that is not in stack because it was popped by an even smaller value to its right? We argued safe. Let's try to find a concrete counterexample with brute mentally:
Array [3,1,2,0]. For i0 val3, next <=3 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push [3]
i2 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top2>1 pop, top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,1]
i0 val3 top1>3 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top1>0 pop, top0>0 no, nxt=3? Wait nearest <=0 to right of i1 is i3 val0, index3. But there is also i2 val1 not <=0. So nxt=3. push [3,1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top1>1 no, nxt=2? Wait top is index2 val1, value1 <=1, so next <=1 is i2 val1 (equal), but nearest <=1 to right of i1 is i2 val1, yes. push [3,2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,2,1]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val1 push
i2 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top2>0 pop, top1>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,0,2]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val2 push
i2 val0 top2>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [2]
i1 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=2 push [2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Seems fine.

i2 val0 top2>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [2]
i1 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=2 push [2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `prev_less` stack pop `>=` and previous strictly less if there is a smaller element that was popped by an even smaller later? We argued safe. Let's test [1,0,2] for i2 val2 prev_less should i1 val0. Stack after i1: i0 val1 push, i1 val0 pop i0, push i1. Stack [1]. i2 val2 top0>=2 no prev1. Good.
[1,2,0,3] for i3 val3 prev_less should i2 val0. Stack after i2: i0 val1 push, i1 val2 push, i2 val0 pop i1, pop i0, push i2. Stack [2]. i3 val3 prev2. Good.
[2,1,3,2] for i3 val2 prev_less should i1 val1. Stack after i2: i0 val2 push, i1 val1 pop i0 push1, i2 val3 push [1,2]. i3 val2 pop i2, top i1 val1>=2 no prev1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "at most k elements" and using `count_pairs` for each i as rightmost min. Suppose a subarray has rightmost min i, but there is an equal to left. Our `prev_less` ignores equal, so L can be after equal? Actually if equal to left within subarray, L <= equal < i. Condition L > prev_less. Since prev_less is nearest strictly smaller, not equal. If there is equal to left, prev_less could be before L or none. So L can be before equal. That's fine; i is rightmost min, equal to left allowed. The count includes all L in (prev_less, i]. It may include L positions that are before an equal to left? Yes. For subarray [0,1] in [1,1], i1 prev_less=-1, L can be0 or1; L=0 includes equal to left. Good.
Now, if there is an equal to left but also a smaller before L, prev_less < L. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "next_less_equal" if there is an equal to right outside subarray but before R? If equal to right within subarray, then i not rightmost min, so not counted. Condition R < next_less_equal ensures no equal to right in subarray. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "sum of maximum and minimum elements" if values are floats? No, ints.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with code if `nums` is a tuple and `[-x for x in nums]` returns list; okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "k" being a positive integer but maybe larger than n? Constraint says <=. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "nums.length <= 80000" and recursion not used. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "Return the sum" and no modulo; Python int. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] <= 10^6" and answer maybe > 2^63? Let's compute max number subarrays length<=n = n(n+1)/2 = 3.2e9. Each min+max can be up to 2e6 if values all positive? Actually if all positive 1e6, min+max=2e6 per subarray, total ~6.4e15. If values can be negative, min+max could be up to 2e6 (if min=-1e6,max=1e6) or down to -2e6. Absolute <=2e6. So <=6.4e15 <9.22e18. Fits 64-bit. Python fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "sum of the maximum and minimum elements" maybe they want sum of (max + min) for each subarray, not sum of max plus sum of min? Same.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with using `min_sum` on negated array for max if values are at bounds -10^6, 10^6; no overflow in Python. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "subarrays with at most k elements" and our count_pairs counts subarrays where i is min but length <=k. However, if there are multiple minima, the rightmost min is counted. Does the length constraint depend on chosen i? For a subarray, length is fixed. If rightmost min i, x+y-1 = length. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prev_less" and "next_less_equal" boundaries if there are values equal to current outside subarray but within boundaries. For rightmost min, next_less_equal > R, so no equal to right. Equal to left allowed. prev_less < L, so no smaller to left; equal to left not considered. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "previous strictly less" stack pop `>=` and "next less or equal" stack pop `>` if values are equal and we want rightmost min. We verified.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with `count_pairs` using `a = i - prev[i]` and `b = nxt[i] - i`. For i=0, prev=-1, a=1. For i=n-1, nxt=n, b=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `nxt` array from right-to-left: We initialize `nxt = [n] * n`, then for each i, if stack empty, nxt[i]=n. If stack top after popping has value <= x, nxt[i]=st[-1]. But what if there is a closer index with value <= x that is not in stack because it was popped by an even smaller value to its right? We argued safe. Let's try to find a concrete counterexample with brute mentally:
Array [3,1,2,0]. For i0 val3, next <=3 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push [3]
i2 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top2>1 pop, top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,1]
i0 val3 top1>3 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top1>0 pop, top0>0 no, nxt=3? Wait nearest <=0 to right of i1 is i3 val0, index3. But there is also i2 val1 not <=0. So nxt=3. push [3,1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top1>1 no, nxt=2? Wait top is index2 val1, value1 <=1, so next <=1 is i2 val1 (equal), but nearest <=1 to right of i1 is i2 val1, yes. push [3,2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,2,1]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val1 push
i2 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top2>0 pop, top1>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,0,2]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val2 push
i2 val0 top2>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [2]
i1 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=2 push [2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Seems fine.

i2 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top2>0 pop, top1>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,0,2]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val2 push
i2 val0 top2>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [2]
i1 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=2 push [2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `prev_less` stack pop `>=` and previous strictly less if there is a smaller element that was popped by an even smaller later? We argued safe. Let's test [1,0,2] for i2 val2 prev_less should i1 val0. Stack after i1: i0 val1 push, i1 val0 pop i0, push i1. Stack [1]. i2 val2 top0>=2 no prev1. Good.
[1,2,0,3] for i3 val3 prev_less should i2 val0. Stack after i2: i0 val1 push, i1 val2 push, i2 val0 pop i1, pop i0, push i2. Stack [2]. i3 val3 prev2. Good.
[2,1,3,2] for i3 val2 prev_less should i1 val1. Stack after i2: i0 val2 push, i1 val1 pop i0 push1, i2 val3 push [1,2]. i3 val2 pop i2, top i1 val1>=2 no prev1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "at most k elements" and using `count_pairs` for each i as rightmost min. Suppose a subarray has rightmost min i, but there is an equal to left. Our `prev_less` ignores equal, so L can be after equal? Actually if equal to left within subarray, L <= equal < i. Condition L > prev_less. Since prev_less is nearest strictly smaller, not equal. If there is equal to left, prev_less could be before L or none. So L can be before equal. That's fine; i is rightmost min, equal to left allowed. The count includes all L in (prev_less, i]. It may include L positions that are before an equal to left? Yes. For subarray [0,1] in [1,1], i1 prev_less=-1, L can be0 or1; L=0 includes equal to left. Good.
Now, if there is an equal to left but also a smaller before L, prev_less < L. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "next_less_equal" if there is an equal to right outside subarray but before R? If equal to right within subarray, then i not rightmost min, so not counted. Condition R < next_less_equal ensures no equal to right in subarray. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "sum of maximum and minimum elements" if values are floats? No, ints.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with code if `nums` is a tuple and `[-x for x in nums]` returns list; okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "k" being a positive integer but maybe larger than n? Constraint says <=. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "nums.length <= 80000" and recursion not used. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "Return the sum" and no modulo; Python int. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] <= 10^6" and answer maybe > 2^63? Let's compute max number subarrays length<=n = n(n+1)/2 = 3.2e9. Each min+max can be up to 2e6 if values all positive? Actually if all positive 1e6, min+max=2e6 per subarray, total ~6.4e15. If values can be negative, min+max could be up to 2e6 (if min=-1e6,max=1e6) or down to -2e6. Absolute <=2e6. So <=6.4e15 <9.22e18. Fits 64-bit. Python fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "sum of the maximum and minimum elements" maybe they want sum of (max + min) for each subarray, not sum of max plus sum of min? Same.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with using `min_sum` on negated array for max if values are at bounds -10^6, 10^6; no overflow in Python. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "subarrays with at most k elements" and our count_pairs counts subarrays where i is min but length <=k. However, if there are multiple minima, the rightmost min is counted. Does the length constraint depend on chosen i? For a subarray, length is fixed. If rightmost min i, x+y-1 = length. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prev_less" and "next_less_equal" boundaries if there are values equal to current outside subarray but within boundaries. For rightmost min, next_less_equal > R, so no equal to right. Equal to left allowed. prev_less < L, so no smaller to left; equal to left not considered. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "previous strictly less" stack pop `>=` and "next less or equal" stack pop `>` if values are equal and we want rightmost min. We verified.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with `count_pairs` using `a = i - prev[i]` and `b = nxt[i] - i`. For i=0, prev=-1, a=1. For i=n-1, nxt=n, b=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `nxt` array from right-to-left: We initialize `nxt = [n] * n`, then for each i, if stack empty, nxt[i]=n. If stack top after popping has value <= x, nxt[i]=st[-1]. But what if there is a closer index with value <= x that is not in stack because it was popped by an even smaller value to its right? We argued safe. Let's try to find a concrete counterexample with brute mentally:
Array [3,1,2,0]. For i0 val3, next <=3 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push [3]
i2 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top2>1 pop, top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,1]
i0 val3 top1>3 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top1>0 pop, top0>0 no, nxt=3? Wait nearest <=0 to right of i1 is i3 val0, index3. But there is also i2 val1 not <=0. So nxt=3. push [3,1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top1>1 no, nxt=2? Wait top is index2 val1, value1 <=1, so next <=1 is i2 val1 (equal), but nearest <=1 to right of i1 is i2 val1, yes. push [3,2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,2,1]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val1 push
i2 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top2>0 pop, top1>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,0,2]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val2 push
i2 val0 top2>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [2]
i1 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=2 push [2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Seems fine.

i1 val1 top1>1 no, nxt=2? Wait top is index2 val1, value1 <=1, so next <=1 is i2 val1 (equal), but nearest <=1 to right of i1 is i2 val1, yes. push [3,2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,2,1]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val1 push
i2 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top2>0 pop, top1>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,0,2]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val2 push
i2 val0 top2>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [2]
i1 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=2 push [2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `prev_less` stack pop `>=` and previous strictly less if there is a smaller element that was popped by an even smaller later? We argued safe. Let's test [1,0,2] for i2 val2 prev_less should i1 val0. Stack after i1: i0 val1 push, i1 val0 pop i0, push i1. Stack [1]. i2 val2 top0>=2 no prev1. Good.
[1,2,0,3] for i3 val3 prev_less should i2 val0. Stack after i2: i0 val1 push, i1 val2 push, i2 val0 pop i1, pop i0, push i2. Stack [2]. i3 val3 prev2. Good.
[2,1,3,2] for i3 val2 prev_less should i1 val1. Stack after i2: i0 val2 push, i1 val1 pop i0 push1, i2 val3 push [1,2]. i3 val2 pop i2, top i1 val1>=2 no prev1. Good.
Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "at most k elements" and using `count_pairs` for each i as rightmost min. Suppose a subarray has rightmost min i, but there is an equal to left. Our `prev_less` ignores equal, so L can be after equal? Actually if equal to left within subarray, L <= equal < i. Condition L > prev_less. Since prev_less is nearest strictly smaller, not equal. If there is equal to left, prev_less could be before L or none. So L can be before equal. That's fine; i is rightmost min, equal to left allowed. The count includes all L in (prev_less, i]. It may include L positions that are before an equal to left? Yes. For subarray [0,1] in [1,1], i1 prev_less=-1, L can be0 or1; L=0 includes equal to left. Good.
Now, if there is an equal to left but also a smaller before L, prev_less < L. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "next_less_equal" if there is an equal to right outside subarray but before R? If equal to right within subarray, then i not rightmost min, so not counted. Condition R < next_less_equal ensures no equal to right in subarray. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "sum of maximum and minimum elements" if values are floats? No, ints.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with code if `nums` is a tuple and `[-x for x in nums]` returns list; okay.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "k" being a positive integer but maybe larger than n? Constraint says <=. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "nums.length <= 80000" and recursion not used. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "Return the sum" and no modulo; Python int. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "nums[i] <= 10^6" and answer maybe > 2^63? Let's compute max number subarrays length<=n = n(n+1)/2 = 3.2e9. Each min+max can be up to 2e6 if values all positive? Actually if all positive 1e6, min+max=2e6 per subarray, total ~6.4e15. If values can be negative, min+max could be up to 2e6 (if min=-1e6,max=1e6) or down to -2e6. Absolute <=2e6. So <=6.4e15 <9.22e18. Fits 64-bit. Python fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "sum of the maximum and minimum elements" maybe they want sum of (max + min) for each subarray, not sum of max plus sum of min? Same.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with using `min_sum` on negated array for max if values are at bounds -10^6, 10^6; no overflow in Python. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with "subarrays with at most k elements" and our count_pairs counts subarrays where i is min but length <=k. However, if there are multiple minima, the rightmost min is counted. Does the length constraint depend on chosen i? For a subarray, length is fixed. If rightmost min i, x+y-1 = length. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "prev_less" and "next_less_equal" boundaries if there are values equal to current outside subarray but within boundaries. For rightmost min, next_less_equal > R, so no equal to right. Equal to left allowed. prev_less < L, so no smaller to left; equal to left not considered. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "previous strictly less" stack pop `>=` and "next less or equal" stack pop `>` if values are equal and we want rightmost min. We verified.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible issue with `count_pairs` using `a = i - prev[i]` and `b = nxt[i] - i`. For i=0, prev=-1, a=1. For i=n-1, nxt=n, b=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `nxt` array from right-to-left: We initialize `nxt = [n] * n`, then for each i, if stack empty, nxt[i]=n. If stack top after popping has value <= x, nxt[i]=st[-1]. But what if there is a closer index with value <= x that is not in stack because it was popped by an even smaller value to its right? i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,1,1,0]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val1. Scan:
i3 val0 push
i2 val1 top0>1 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val1 top1>1 no, nxt=2? Wait top is index2 val1, value1 <=1, so next <=1 is i2 val1 (equal), but nearest <=1 to right of i1 is i2 val1, yes. push [3,2,1]
i0 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=1. Good.
Array [2,0,2,1]. For i0 val2, next <=2 should i1 val0. Scan:
i3 val1 push
i2 val2 top1>2 no, nxt=3 push [3,2]
i1 val0 top2>0 pop, top1>0 pop, empty nxt=4 push [1]
i0 val2 top0>2 no, nxt=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with `prev_less` stack pop `>=` and previous strictly less if there is a smaller element that was popped by an even smaller later? We argued safe. Let's test [1,0,2] for i2 val2 prev_less should i1 val0. Stack after i1: i0 val1 push, i1 val0 pop i0, push i1. Stack [1]. i2 val2 top0>=2 no prev1. Good.

Seems fine.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "at most k elements" and using `count_pairs` for each i as rightmost min. Suppose a subarray has rightmost min i, but there is an equal to left. Our `prev_less` ignores equal, so L can be after equal? Actually if equal to left within subarray, L <= equal < i. Condition L > prev_less. Since prev_less is nearest strictly smaller, not equal. If there is equal to left, prev_less could be before L or none. So L can be before equal. That's fine; i is rightmost min, equal to left allowed. The count includes all L in (prev_less, i]. It may include L positions that are before an equal to left? Yes. For subarray [0,1] in [1,1], i1 prev_less=-1, L can be0 or1; L=0 includes equal to left. Good.
Now, if there is an equal to left but also a smaller before L, prev_less < L. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible issue with "next_less_equal" if there is an equal to right outside subarray but before R? If equal to right within subarray, then i not rightmost min, so not counted. Condition R < next_less_equal ensures no equal to right in subarray. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minMaxSubarraySum(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        def tri(s: int) -> int:
            return s * (s - 1) // 2 if s > 1 else 0

        def count_pairs(a: int, b: int) -> int:
            t = k + 1
            return tri(t) - tri(t - a) - tri(t - b) + tri(t - a - b)

        def min_sum(arr: List[int]) -> int:
            n = len(arr)

            prev = [-1] * n
            st = []
            for i, x in enumerate(arr):
                while st and arr[st[-1]] >= x:
                    st.pop()
                prev[i] = st[-1] if st else -1
                st.append(i)

            nxt = [n] * n
            st = []
            for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
                x = arr[i]
                while st and arr[st[-1]] > x:
                    st.pop()
                nxt[i] = st[-1] if st else n
                st.append(i)

            total = 0
            for i, x in enumerate(arr):
                a = i - prev[i]
                b = nxt[i] - i
                total += x * count_pairs(a, b)

            return total

        return min_sum(nums) - min_sum([-x for x in nums])
```
